Other times I feel really slowed down, like even grabbing menus or walking over to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're losing energy easily or feeling fatigued, even if you haven't done anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired after work, but that seems normal with being on my feet. I haven’t noticed feeling wiped out for no reason, not in a way that really stands out.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely lost pleasure. I still do things sometimes, and there are moments they’re okay. It’s more that I don’t feel much from them lately, but I’m not sure it’s totally gone.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say no pleasure at all. It’s more muted, I guess. Like if I make myself do something I used to like, sometimes it’s still okay, but it doesn’t really hit the same. I don’t think it’s completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t care about doing things you used to care about, like even small things that used to make you feel good, like finishing a task or spending time with someone close to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have noticed that. Like at work, I used to joke around with the regulars more, and after shifts I’d text my brother or answer him pretty quick. Now I mostly just keep my face straight, say what I need to say, and let messages sit there. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude, I just don’t really feel the push to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to really enjoy, like spending time with people close to you or doing certain activities, don’t bring you any pleasure or satisfaction anymore — even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say they bring me no pleasure at all. It’s more like… less than before. If I make myself do something or see someone, it can be fine, but I don’t really get much out of it the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you now get little or no pleasure from almost all activities that used to bring you joy, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it as almost all activities. Some things still feel okay if I actually do them. It’s just… the enjoyment is lower than it used to be, and I don’t always feel like bothering with it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in things you used to care about, so much so that doing them feels like just going through the motions, even when you push yourself to do them?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say completely lost interest, no. It’s more like I just don’t feel much pull toward things. Like with the regulars at work, I used to joke around more, and now I mostly keep a straight face and say what I have to. And with my brother, I’ll see his texts after a shift and just let them sit there. It feels like effort, even if I know I should answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

Messages sit there because I don’t really feel like answering, even when I know I should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or waking up earlier than you should?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear change with my sleep, at least not enough that I’ve been keeping track of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re a failure or that you’ve done something wrong, maybe even feeling guilty about things you wouldn’t usually feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s a big part of why I came in. I keep feeling like I let my family down by taking on this mortgage, and then at work, even a small mistake feels like proof that I’m useless. It’s like I deserve whatever happens after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Most days I feel pretty down, like there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. I still go through the motions at work, carrying plates and smiling when I’m supposed to, but it doesn’t really feel real or good.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I think so. People at work have pointed it out. Some shifts I’m pacing around, checking the same tables or receipts over and over like I can’t settle. Other times I feel really slowed down, like even grabbing menus or walking over to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're losing energy easily or feeling fatigued, even if you haven't done anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired after work, but that seems normal with being on my feet. I haven’t noticed feeling wiped out for no reason, not in a way that really stands out.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely lost pleasure. It’s more that I don’t feel much from them lately, but I’m not sure it’s totally gone.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you no longer get any pleasure or satisfaction from things you used to enjoy, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say no pleasure at all. I don’t think it’s completely gone.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you don’t care about doing things you used to care about, like even small things that used to make you feel good, like finishing a task or spending time with someone close to you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have noticed that. Now I mostly just keep my face straight, say what I need to say, and let messages sit there. I used to joke around with the regulars at work, and after my shifts I’d usually text my brother back. Lately I just kind of keep a straight face, say what I need to say, and leave it at that. I haven’t noticed a clear change with my sleep, at least not enough that I’ve been keeping track of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re a failure or that you’ve done something wrong, maybe even feeling guilty about things you wouldn’t usually feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s a big part of why I came in. I keep feeling like I let my family down by taking on this mortgage, and then at work, even a small mistake feels like proof that I’m useless. It’s like I deserve whatever happens after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Most days I feel pretty down, like there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. People at work have pointed it out. Some shifts I’m pacing around, checking the same tables or receipts over and over like I can’t settle. Other times I feel really slowed down, like even grabbing menus or walking over to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're losing energy easily or feeling fatigued, even if you haven't done anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t noticed a clear change with my sleep, at least not enough that I’ve been keeping track of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you’re a failure or that you’ve done something wrong, maybe even feeling guilty about things you wouldn’t usually feel guilty about?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s a big part of why I came in. I keep feeling like I let my family down by taking on this mortgage, and then at work, even a small mistake feels like proof that I’m useless. It’s like I deserve whatever happens after that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah. Most days I feel pretty down, like there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. People at work have pointed it out. Some shifts I’m pacing around, checking the same tables or receipts over and over like I can’t settle. Other times I feel really slowed down, like even grabbing menus or walking over to the POS takes me longer than it should.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you're losing energy easily or feeling fatigued, even if you haven't done anything physically or mentally demanding?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I get tired after work, but that seems normal with being on my feet. I haven’t noticed feeling wiped out for no reason, not in a way that really stands out.\n\nDoctor: Have you completely lost pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with family and friends?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say completely lost pleasure. Now I mostly just keep my face straight, say what I need to say, and let messages sit there. It’s not that I’m trying to be rude, I just don’t really feel the push to do it.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that things you used to really enjoy, like spending time with people close to you or doing certain activities, don’t bring you any pleasure or satisfaction anymore — even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say they bring me no pleasure at all. It’s more like… less than before. If I make myself do something or see someone, it can be fine, but I don’t really get much out of it the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you now get little or no pleasure from almost all activities that used to bring you joy, even when you make yourself do them?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d put it as almost all activities.
